- **Built:** domain files `appwrite/schema/{catalogue,records,assessments}.json` (8 + 7 + 4 = 19 tables): catalogue `curriculum, catalogue_version, level, level_equivalence, subject, strand, topic, struggle_tag`; records `session_log, session_log_topic, session_log_struggle, topic_progress, school_upload, handover_summary, handover_rating`; assessments `question, assessment_blueprint, assessment, assessment_item`. `appwrite/catalogue_rules.py` (executable reference, stdlib, no I/O: `assert_editable`, `edit_catalogue_row`/`add_catalogue_row`/`soft_delete_catalogue_row`, `publish_version`/`retire_version`/`copy_version_as_draft`, derived-key builders, `submit_session_log`, `upsert_topic_progress`/`derive_progress`/`rebuild_topic_progress`, `assert_topic_in_child_version`, two-person rule `assert_two_person`/`approve_question`/`validate_question_row`, `assert_marker_allowed`), migration `0005_catalogue_schema.py` (applies the three domains, no rows), buckets `school-reports` and `question-media` in `appwrite.config.json` (fileSecurity, encryption, antivirus, no client permissions), generated config and `docs/erd/{catalogue,records,assessments}.md` (46 tables total), README section "Catalogue, logs and assessments (M03)". Edited `tests/test_schema.py` (one line: the duplicate-table test picked `domains[1]`, now looked up by name).
- **Decisions:** (1) Immutability: curators and clients have no write permission on any table (empty table permissions, validator-enforced); every catalogue write goes through the core-service rules above, which reject any edit/add/soft-delete under a published or retired version (`VersionImmutable`); only draft is editable; new wording = copy as draft, publish. The issue's "how we'll know" asks for this test, so the pure validator `assert_editable` is included. (2) `published_key` = curriculum id while published: at most one published version per curriculum; publishing retires the previous one, children keep their version so old versions keep resolving. (3) Soft-delete rule applied: every unique index on a soft table is over derived `*_key` columns (curriculum code, version, level/subject/topic codes, level pair, struggle label, one log per session, log topic/struggle, blueprint, item); `topic_progress` is `hard` (derived, rebuildable) so its `(child_id, topic_id)` unique is raw and literal as the AC says; `handover_summary`/`handover_rating` are `immutable` (unique `source_key` makes generation idempotent, unique (summary, teacher)). No new `SOFT_UNIQUE_LEGACY` entries. (4) Renames forced by the validator's money-word regex (`rate`): `handover_summary.generated_at` -> `snapshot_at`, `handover_rating.rated_by_teacher_id`/`rated_at` -> `teacher_id`/`created_at`. (5) `strand` added (not in SR 5.3 or the issue; live has it, `topic.strand_id` optional). (6) `struggle_tag` is not versioned (SR 5.3; live has `catalogue_version_id`) and is retired with `active=false`. (7) `topic` carries `catalogue_version_id` (SR lists subject_id, level_id; the issue requires version membership); same-version consistency of subject/level/strand/parent is a core rule (`check_topic_refs`). (8) Question: `options`/`answer_key` JSON text, `image_file_id`/`audio_file_id` Storage ids (non_fk), approver must differ from author (core rule + data validator, the schema cannot compare columns) and be a curator/senior teacher (core role check); approved questions are retired, not edited; `answer_key` never sent to the parent app before submit. ASM-7 marker not the child's teacher: `assert_marker_allowed`. (9) Log submit is idempotent per session; `topic_progress` update is a second write (no Appwrite transaction), repaired by `rebuild_topic_progress`; stale evidence (edit of an older session) never regresses newer progress. (10) Child data tables carry no table-level read; per-row grants are M05/M06 (note appended to #40).

- **Live diff (names only; snapshot not committed), M03 entities** (`diff_live.py <snapshot> --only ...`): live has `curriculum, catalogue_version, level, level_equivalence, subject, strand, topic, struggle_tag` (8); lacks all of `session_log, session_log_topic, session_log_struggle, topic_progress, school_upload, handover_summary, handover_rating, question, assessment_blueprint, assessment, assessment_item` (11). Differences on the 8: live tables have table-level `read(label:curator|opsadmin|superadmin)` (against ADR 0012; repo has none, reads via core service); live has no `deleted_at` or derived `*_key` columns and uses raw unique indexes (`curriculum.code`, `(catalogue_version_id, code)` on level/subject, `(curriculum_id, version)`, `pair_key` required, `(subject_id, level_id, code)` on topic); `curriculum.code` is an enum live (repo varchar(16)); `catalogue_version`: live has `created_at` (live-only), repo adds `retired_at`, `published_key` (one published per curriculum); `created_by_id` exists on both, only `created_at` is live-only; live `level_equivalence.pair_key` required size 73, repo optional derived size 80; `struggle_tag`: live is per `catalogue_version_id` and `active` required, repo is unversioned per SR; live code sizes 20 (level, subject) and 40 (topic), repo 32/32/40; live `topic` has a unique on (subject, level, code) without the version (a new version could not reuse codes live: repo keys include the version); index sets differ (repo adds `(version, subject, level, sort_order)` for the topic list). Recommendation: merge with the repo as base; live `curriculum` has 3 rows (read-only): migrate them into the repo shape on a staging project first. Nothing applied to live.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) K02-K04 curator tools and the core session-log service port `catalogue_rules.py`; (2) M05/M06 per-row read grants for logs, uploads, handover summaries, assessments and a table-level guard so only the core service key writes catalogue tables; (3) retention job must hard-delete soft rows of these tables and `topic_progress`/`school_upload` files; (4) owner data: curriculum codes and catalogue content (no seed); (5) marker/approver role checks (`curator` or senior teacher) belong to the core role layer.

- **Behaviour:** `send(event_key, Recipient(user_id, role, locale), variables, idempotency_key, deep_link=, not_after=)` renders every channel BEFORE creating rows (template error: log + alert `notification_template_error` + blocked rows, then raises; safeguarding instead sends a built-in generic text). One `notification` row per channel, id = hash(idempotency key, event, user, channel) (create = atomic claim; Conflict = duplicate). Priorities: critical (safeguarding + substitute assigned, session cancelled, payment failed: never deferred, never rate-limited, push failure/no device falls back to email row `<dedupe>:fallback`, push row status `fallback`), operational (push deferred in quiet hours unless `urgent`: assignment offer, 1h reminder), marketing (mutable). Deferred = `queued` with future `scheduled_at` (public status `deferred`), suppressed = `blocked` with `last_error` `suppressed:<reason>` (public `suppressed`). Crash recovery: row `queued` with lease (`NOTIFY_LEASE_S`); a retry after the lease resumes with the SAME provider idempotency key (row id + attempt group; group only increments after a definite transient failure), so the provider dedupes. In-process per-key and per-row locks. Retries: transient backoff base*2^(n-1), `NOTIFY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`. `record_delivery(notification_id, delivered|bounced|bounced_soft|complained, at, reason)` idempotent and order-safe (late delivered never reopens failed). Critical email failure/hard bounce -> alert `notification_delivery_failed`. Rate limit per recipient+template+channel counted from rows (approximate under races). `send_safeguarding(event, lead, backup, vars, key)` sends push+email+in_app to both at once, arms `EscalationHooks.arm(group_id, lead_id, backup_id, after)` (interface only; Null/Fake), alert `safeguarding_no_backup`. `notification_owner_id(row)` for M05; row read permission `read(user:<id>)`.

- **Protocol (chosen, justified in docs/ops/audit.md):** audit-first. Intent row (deterministic id from request/job key + entity + id + n) created BEFORE the business write (audit store down = change refused); seen failure adds a `failed` outcome row; crash settled by `audit-recover` (entity state hash vs intent before/after hash -> `not_applied` row, or counted `unverifiable`, never guessed). Happy path = exactly one row. No schema change (envelope JSON in existing `before`/`after`, meta in `after`).

- **Reviewer findings (round 1), N01 (branch rebased locally on main 9ad316b, nothing pushed, no PR opened).** Commands, clean worktree: `cd services/core && uv sync && uv run pytest -q` 1116 passed; notification tests x5: 130 passed each; ruff check + format clean; `secrets_check.py` ok (85) and `--write` no diff; `make setup`, `make lint` (11 ok, 2 skipped, 0 failed), `make test` (13 ran ok, 0 skipped, 0 failed), `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests` 189 OK, `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` all passed. Seeded catalog checked row by row against PRD 7.13: all rows, channels, recipients and priority classes match (note: the catalog has 26 events = 25 PRD events + 1 sample marketing event, not 27: fix the count in docs/memory). Quiet hours at 20:59:59/21:00:00/06:59:59/07:00:00 +03:00, UTC input, month/year/leap rollover: correct; naive datetimes raise. Preferences: every combination of marketing_muted/muted_channels x categories (operational, safeguarding, unknown, empty, upper-case) never blocks a non-marketing row (unknown categories are not-mutable: safe); catalog events cannot be re-categorised; `set_muted_channels` raises `NotPersistable` and is not called on any service path. Publish needs `superadmin` in code; versions are insert-only. Injection: HTML/`{{`/`{%`/`${` in values are escaped or inert (single pass), CR/LF, U+2028, RTL override, 201 chars rejected, Arabic accepted; error text names variables only, never values; no email/token/variable in logs. Concurrency: 120 deferred rows, 3 threads dispatching, 120 deliveries exactly. Safeguarding at 22:30 with push failing: email+in_app sent at once to lead and backup, escalation armed once. Deployed wiring uses Unconfigured channels (permanent failure, alert for critical), no silent success. Mutations I ran (each fails tests): skip quiet hours, allow muting safeguarding, drop critical fallback (`and ev.fallback_email` -> `and False`), critical rate-limited, random push provider key per attempt (double send), unescaped HTML. One mutation SURVIVES: random per-attempt EMAIL provider key (see 4).
  1. **BLOCKER: a provider call that raises (timeout, connection error) wedges the dispatcher and defeats max attempts.** `_dispatch`/`_deliver` only handle returned `ChannelResult`; any exception from `push.send`/`email.send` (what a real HTTP adapter does on timeout) propagates. Repro (scratchpad `repro/r2.py`, `r3.py`): push channel raising `TimeoutError` for one user: `send()` raises to the caller after the row exists (the in_app row is never created in that call); the row stays `queued` with a lease; after each lease expiry `dispatch_due` raises again and `attempts` grows past `NOTIFY_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (9 after 8 runs, never `failed`, no fallback, no alert); because rows are ordered by `scheduled_at` and the poisoned row sorts first every time, healthy rows behind it are never dispatched (0 of the healthy deliveries in 3 runs). This violates "retry after provider timeout", "transient vs permanent", "max 4 attempts" and job error isolation, and for a critical event (payment failed, safeguarding push) it means no fallback email. Fix: wrap each channel send in `_deliver` (and the template render) in `try/except Exception` -> `ChannelResult(False, TRANSIENT, detail=type name only)` (never `str(e)`: may contain addresses/tokens) so `_finish` counts the attempt; isolate each row in `dispatch_due` (`try/except Exception`, log id + exception type, continue); same for `_resume` in `send`. Tests: raising push, raising email, raising in_app; poisoned row does not block healthy rows; attempts stop at max and a critical row falls back/alerts.
  2. **BLOCKER (data retention): `expire_payloads` stalls after 120 rows.** It lists the oldest `page*3` rows by `created_at` with no `deleted_at` filter, skips already soft-deleted ones, so once the 120 oldest are soft-deleted it returns 0 forever and later rows keep their payload (variables, i.e. names/amounts/times) past retention. Repro (`repro/r5.py`): 200 sends = 600 rows: calls return 40, 40, 40, 0, 0...; 480 rows still hold payload. Fix: filter on `deleted_at` is null in the query (or page by cursor), test with more rows than page*3 and across several calls, and with the boundary date (`created_at == before` kept).
  3. Non-blocking, fix in the same round if cheap: (a) test gap: the EMAIL provider idempotency key on crash retry is untested (mutation `f"{rid}:{uuid4}"` for the email key passes all 130 tests): add an email crash-after-accept test like the push one. (b) No per-template TTL: stale notifications ('session in 1 hour', 'tutor checked in') are delivered late if the dispatch job is down for hours, unless every caller passes `not_after`; add a default `ttl` per `EventDef` (urgent and reminder events short) applied when `not_after` is None; a naive `not_after` is silently read as server-local time (reject it). (c) FCM v1 has no idempotency key, so a crash between provider accept and the status update (lease window) can produce one duplicate PUSH; email dedupes only if Resend honours the key (ADR 0005 says verify in N02). State both honestly in the docs and N02 ticket (push: at-least-once, duplicate bounded to one per token per crash). (d) `EmailChannel` does not validate `to` for CR/LF; the future directory/N02 adapter must (note on #127). (e) the 5 per 10 min limit also suppresses legitimate operational rows (e.g. 6 payment_received for several children): owner default, documented as approximate; the Query limit(200) can undercount for very busy users. (f) `render_notification` on a row after `expire_payloads` raises MissingVariables: the inbox API must skip expired rows.
  Owner-blocked and acceptable: copy approval, FCM/APNs/Resend accounts (N02), urgent/critical lists, quiet hours global, rate/attempt/escalation defaults, per-channel marketing opt-out column. Next: builder fixes 1 and 2 (+ tests, + 3a, 3b if cheap), rebases on main, force-with-lease, sets ready-for-review; reviewer re-runs and opens the PR.

- **Reviewer round 1 (M06):** verified on a fresh worktree rebased on main 7916205: core pytest 1326 passed (audit tests 345 passed x3), ruff clean, secrets_check ok 80 entries (+--write no diff), make setup/lint (11 ok 2 skipped)/test (13 ok 0 failed), scripts unittest 189 OK, pre-commit all passed. Nine own mutations (update skips audit, no redaction, failed not marked, recovery never not_applied, reason unenforced, no truncation, double write on retry, read unlogged, audit after write) were all caught. Role matrix of `list_audit` correct (opsadmin/superadmin always, safeguardinglead only with flag, all others and unknown/upper-case labels 403). Audit-write failure => business write not attempted (AuditUnavailable, state unchanged). Concurrent identical request: one row. Retry before/after recovery: no duplicate. Lookback expiry (7 h): intent stays unsettled, widening via payload settles it (documented). Docs honest about tripwire misses. Blocking findings, all reproduced with scripts:
  1. **Client-controlled dedupe key = audit bypass.** `main.py` takes `X-Request-ID` from the client; `AuditContext.key_base` = request id; `_begin` treats an existing intent (or `recorded` row) with the same key as a replay and writes NO new row. Repro: two different `update("tier","t1",...)` with request id `same-rid` => both applied (status paused then closed), ONE audit row (second change unaudited). Same for case reads: user A and user B read `safeguarding_case` c1 with the same request id => one `read` row (A only); B's read is unlogged. Any authenticated caller can switch audit off for an entity/case by sending a fixed `X-Request-ID`. Fix: the replay key must not be client-chosen (server-generated request id for audit, or include actor + a hash of the intended after-state in the key; on replay verify the intent's `after_state`/actor match, else treat as a new change), plus tests: same rid, different payload/actor => separate rows; same for `record`/`read_audited`.
  2. **Ambiguous errors recorded as `failed`.** Any ordinary exception from the business write (timeout/`RepositoryUnavailable` after the server applied it) gets a `failed` outcome row though the change landed (repro: write lands then raises RuntimeError: row says failed, state changed). The log then claims a change did not happen. Fix: only mark `failed` for errors that prove nothing was written (Conflict/validation/NotFound, 4xx); for unavailable/timeout/unknown leave the intent for `audit-recover`; test with FaultyRepository 'after' + ordinary error.
  3. **Reason check accepts invisible text.** `\u200b`, `\u200b\u200b`, `\ufeff`, braille blank `\u2800` pass `ReasonRequired` (`str.strip()` does not remove them) for `tier.override` etc. (issue/brief: zero-width must count as blank). Fix: strip Unicode format/control/space (Cf, Cc, Z*, U+2800) before the blank check, and apply the same to the stored reason; test each.
  4. **H09/$01/N01 hooks not delivered and not recorded.** The #41 requirement (real sink for the admin search `AuditSink`, delete `UnwiredAuditSink`, Staging/Production refuse search while unwired) is untouched: no endpoint (no audit route exists at all; `api.py` only has the context dependency), nothing in `apps/admin`, nothing in docs/ops/audit.md or this block. Same for the $01 `ledger.audit.AuditSink` (idempotent on `ledger:<tx id>`) and N01 `TemplateStore.audit`: no implementation and not listed as follow-ups. Either deliver (a core endpoint, e.g. POST audit of `admin.search` with ip/request id from the trusted helpers, authorised by the admin service role, plus the admin-side real sink and guard) or record precisely as REQUIRED before staging on #41/#138, in this block and in docs/ops/audit.md (what exists, what is missing, who builds it), including the three ledger/notification hooks.
  Non-blocking notes: (a) unverifiable intents only produce a log warning: no alert/metric/count surfaced (wire to `AlertSink` or the F08 alert tag); intents older than the lookback are neither counted nor alerted; (b) `list_audit` reads are not themselves audited; (c) key-based redaction misses generic keys such as `coordinates`, `location`, `pickup`, `pin`, `card_pan`, `nin`, `guardian`, `comment`, `title` (only strings pass the F08 scrubber); `subscription.cancel_reason`, `assignment.end_reason`, `earning.note` (parent/staff free text) are stored scrubbed only: decide explicitly in `ENTITY_REDACT`/`TEXT_ALLOWED`; (d) `accounts.py` `_record_grant`: the `audited = ...` line sits above the docstring (docstring is now dead); (e) `child`, `consent_record`, `match_*`, `notification_template` are not audited tables: confirm by decision, record why. No PR opened.

### V01 · Teacher app: application flow (#56)
- Status: **ready-for-review (round 3)**. Branch `v01-teacher-application` (six `V01:` commits, rebased on origin/main at push, `--force-with-lease`, PR not opened). Round 3 fixes (reviewer round 2): 1) BLOCKER takeover lock-out: claims are no longer UPDATED. A claim chain (rows sharing `bucket`) is extended by ONE create whose id is derived from the latest row (`row_id(prev_id)`), so racing takeovers have one winner and a crash at any point leaves nothing half-done: the next request reads the latest row again (same key resumes, other key waits out the window, then chains). Claim life is 7 days (revision claims 1 day), removed by `application-claims-cleanup`; successor ids do not depend on older rows existing. Same chain for `_claim_revision` (double fault fixed). Tests: reviewer's crash harness adopted (`tests/test_teacher_application_crash.py`, 236 cases: crash after every write of a submit, of a takeover, retries with same/new key at now/+61 s/+3 h, 50-thread takeovers, slow old writer, cleanup, revision double/triple fault). 2) BLOCKER JWT: `AppwriteAuthService(onSessionChanged:)` fires on sign-out (first and in finally), sign-in, `verifyOtp`, revoked/expired session; the teacher app wires it to `CachedTokenProvider.invalidate`; cache ignores a backwards clock jump; tests in `session_test.dart` (sign out then another user = fresh token; in-flight fetch before sign-out not cached; revoked session; clock skew; concurrent callers). The parent app does not use a JWT provider. 3) nits: rejected photo fields (`file_id`, state other than chosen) are reported in `issues` but never stored; the ops notice goes to the ROLE recipient `role:opsadmin` (`OPS_QUEUE_RECIPIENT`), line appended to N03 (#128); 360x640 layout test at 100% and 200%. Mutations (each fails): takeover creates duplicates (132 failures), takeover never resumed (113), `extra=forbid` removed so `status` is accepted (10), JWT hook removed (5 Dart failures), plus round 2's file_id/permission mutations. M05 round-1 blocker verified fixed: my own harness (service submit against the permission-aware world for a brand-new teacher and for the sample teacher with an admin-created profile and an open case) shows every created row carries exactly `desired_permissions`, DOB/school/referee contacts appear only in owner+ops rows (teacher_profile gets gender and radius only); mutating `_perms` or removing a SPECS entry fails tests. Crop: removing `x + size <= 1` is acceptable (size is a fraction of the SHORTER edge, so the correct bound needs the image dimensions the server never has; every value is bounded 0..1, NaN/inf rejected by pydantic); V02 must clamp when it applies the crop. Crash injection after every write then a takeover by a new key converges with rows identical to an uninterrupted submit; 50 threads over two new keys: one winner, no duplicates; takeover by another teacher cannot touch the application; slow old writer vs takeover (paused after each of 19 writes): no duplicates, old writer gets 409 `already_submitted` (no fencing but idempotent ids make it safe).
- **Round 2 review findings (must fix):**
  1. **BLOCKER lock-out after a failed takeover.** `_claim_submit`: the takeover marker `appsubmit-takeover:<tid>:<attempt>:<old created_at>` is created, then the old claim row is updated. If the process dies or the repository errors between those two writes (reproduced by crash injection), the old claim keeps its old `created_at` and key, the marker exists forever, and EVERY later attempt by any key (including the takeover key itself) hits the marker Conflict and gets 409 `submit_in_progress`; the claim is never cleaned (`expires_at` is 10 years), so a teacher who lost their key is locked out for good. Suggest: on Conflict read the marker; if it carries the same key resume (finish the claim update); if it is older than the takeover window chain a new marker id that includes the marker's own created_at/key; test: crash at each of the 2 takeover writes, then retry with the same and with a third key.
  2. **BLOCKER cached JWT survives sign-out / user switch.** `CachedTokenProvider.invalidate` is only wired to the 401 retry in `apps/teacher/lib/main.dart`; nothing calls it on `AuthService.signOut()` (`appwrite_auth_service.dart:123`) or after `verifyOtp`/`signIn` for another account, so for up to 9.5 minutes the next user on the same device sends the PREVIOUS user's JWT (their draft saves and submit act as the previous user; the server trusts the token). Fix: invalidate on sign-out and on every new sign-in (or key the cache by session/user id), with a shared-package test that signs out/in as another user and asserts the provider fetches a new token (and that an in-flight fetch from before sign-out is discarded; the generation counter already supports this).
  3. (should fix, same class as 1, needs two faults) `_claim_revision`: a stale revision claim is retaken with the derived id `approvrev-retake:<tid>:<rev>:<old created_at>`; if the retaker also dies after creating that marker and before the row update, every later draft save or submit at that revision gets 409 `draft_conflict` forever (reproduced: crash, +61 s, crash, +3 h, still conflict). Chain the retake id on the marker's own created_at like in 1.
  4. (nit, follow-up) a draft with a photo `file_id` is refused only by an `issues` entry: the value is still stored in the owner's `content_json`; V02 must never read it (or strip it in `normalise_content`). The ops notice has `user_id=None` so even when N03 adds the event it has no recipient (added to the N03 note is the event; recipient is for N03/V04). Reviewer harness files (not committed): `scratchpad/rev-harness/zz_rev_*.py`.

Wired in `main.py` (`create_app(teacher_application=...)`), `jobs/schedule.py`. Role guard: `teacher` label only (parent/admin 403).
- **Schema:** new domain `appwrite/schema/teacher_application.json` + migration `0008_teacher_application_schema.py` (renumber if another branch takes 0008): `teacher_application` (row id = user id; typed VER-1 columns + `content_json`; status draft/submitted/under_review/action_needed/approved/not_approved; deletion `history`), `teacher_referee`, `teacher_application_subject`, `teacher_application_claim` (server-only claims: submit, revision, rate-limit slots). Live has `teacher_referee`/`teacher_referee_check`; ours is a fresh design, V04 owns the check table. Submit writes M01 tables: `teacher_profile` (status `pending`, never active; schema has no `submitted`), `teacher_zone`, `availability_slot`, `teacher_approval` REQUESTS (approved_at/approved_by empty).
- **Design:** every one-of-N is a claim row with a derived id (ADR 0002 idiom). Submit order: claim(teacher, attempt) -> write final rows with deterministic ids -> audit -> notify -> flip `status=submitted` LAST; a crash leaves a draft and a retry with the same key finishes with no duplicates; a different key gets 409 `already_submitted`. Draft saves use `base_revision` (409 `draft_conflict` returns the server draft). Reapply: `not_approved` -> `POST /reopen` only after `TEACHER_APP_REAPPLY_COOLDOWN_DAYS` since `decided_at`; `action_needed` is editable and resubmits as attempt 2. Referee phones use A01's `normalize_ug_phone`; referee == applicant's own number is refused. National ID numbers are NOT collected (SR T-05 = images, V02). `current_school` and DOB are private to owner/admin; audit entries carry ids and counts only; logs carry no PII.
- **Settings (F09 inventory + `.env.example` done):** `TEACHER_APP_MIN_WEEKLY_HOURS` (6), `TEACHER_APP_REAPPLY_COOLDOWN_DAYS` (90), `TEACHER_APP_DRAFT_SAVES_PER_HOUR` (240), `TEACHER_APP_SUBMITS_PER_HOUR` (10).

| Strings externalised, 48 dp, 130%/200% font, no heavy images | flow "every widget string comes from the ARB", "no copy says homeschool"; states "every step renders without overflow at 130% / 200% text", "buttons, chips and steppers are at least 48 dp", "photo preview is decoded at thumbnail size" |
| Tester completes on Android in under 10 minutes and it appears in admin | **owner-blocked** (real device, staging, admin A-05 is V04) |

- **Deviations / honest limits:** (1) `teacher_profile.status` stays `pending` on submit (M01 enum has no submitted); the application row carries `submitted`. (2) Travel limit is `travel_radius_km` (M01 column, T-07 lead text); SR matching and live schema use minutes: question below. (3) Widget tests run on a tall 360x2600 screen because tapping after programmatic scrolling produced false hit-test misses in `flutter_test`; vertical overflow is therefore not exercised, horizontal overflow and text scale are. (4) The shared `AppSpec.teacher.postSignInPath` sends restored sessions to `/apply` always: V03 must gate on status (approved teachers should land on Today). (5) Photo is chosen and cropped but NOT uploaded (V02 uploads it and applies the crop); the real `image_picker` adapter compiles but is untested on a device. (6) The documents step is a placeholder; submission does not require documents until V02 provides `documentsStepProvider`. (7) The real Appwrite repository path (`AppwriteRepository`) and the real `HttpApplicationApi` against the live server are untested; the JWT is taken with `Account.createJWT()` in `main.dart` (compile-checked only). (8) NFC normalisation exists on the server only (Dart has none); lengths are counted in code points on both sides.
- **Dependencies / follow-ups:** K01 catalogue read API (interface `CatalogueReader`/`ReferenceSource`, replace the default); M06 audit logger and N01 notifier (replace `RepositoryAuditSink`/`LogNotifier`); V02 upload (+ documents step, photo upload and crop); V03 status screen and Today gating; V04 admin approval writes `approved_at/approved_by`, referee contact and checks, sets `decided_at`/`not_approved`/`action_needed`; M05 per-user read permissions already set to `read("user:<id>")` on rows created here, admin grants are M05/V04; a Luganda translation of `app_en.arb`.

### H09 · Global search (#138)

- **Status:** **blocked-foundation-merged** (PR #189, squash 4787840; #138 closed). Reviewer: tsc, 250 passed + 1 skipped, eslint, build, `make setup/lint/test`, scripts unittest 189, secrets_check (+--write no diff), pre-commit, PR `ci-ok` all green after rebase on main. Own repros: 16 label subsets x honest/leaky source x in/out-of-scope rows with canary values in unlisted fields (address, coordinates, DOB, school, doc ref, notes, account no.): no leak, superadmin alone 403, curator never queries people, SG only case-linked, masking per role; removing `requireAccess` fails 7 tests; adapter table/column names match merged `appwrite/schema/*.json`; no API key in source/.next/static. Non-blocking notes: (1) no Staging/Production guard for `UnwiredAuditSink` (follow-up appended to #41: real sink, delete Unwired, refuse search in staging/prod while unwired); (2) audit entry lacks ip/request_id that `audit_log` has; (3) SG session scope = all sessions of a case-linked child; (4) ops phone-prefix scanning bounded only by rate cap (min 3 digits, 30/10 s per instance), audit has counts not queries; ops already reads the directory; (5) body read before the 2 KB check; (6) `contains` escaping, indexes, relevance to verify on staging. Previously: ready-for-review · branch `h09-global-search` (commit 01bffa1, rebased on main 431f4ad, pushed --force-with-lease; PR not opened). Owner-blocked: real data, relevance tuning, indexes, audit persistence (M06), SG-scope joins need the staging project / M04 on main.
- **Built (all in `apps/admin`, plus F09 inventory):** `POST /api/search` (`src/app/api/search/route.ts`; POST only so the query never rides in a URL; `await requireAccess("/api/search")`, same-origin check, JSON only, 2 KB body cap, `no-store`); `src/lib/search/{policy,normalize,text,service,limits,types,fake,appwrite,server}.ts`; `src/components/SearchBox.tsx` (ARIA combobox in the top bar, replaces the disabled placeholder); `API_ROUTES` in `roles.ts` (`/api/search` open to ops, curator, safeguarding lead); `APPWRITE_DATABASE_ID` (optional, default `main`) in `config.ts`, `.env.example`, F09 inventory + regenerated `config-matrix.md`.
- **Decisions (evidence in code comments):** per-role allowlist on the server (`policy.ts`): ops = parents, teachers, children, sessions, payments; curator = topics, questions only; safeguarding lead = parents/teachers/children/sessions restricted to rows linked to an open case ("safeguarding" scope) + cases, never payments; superadmin alone = nothing (F06 audit F-3), multi-label = union, broader scope wins. The service asks the source only for allowed types, then re-checks every row (type, scope, screen openable) and maps only whitelisted fields (a leaky source cannot leak: tested). Privacy: phone/email unmasked for opsadmin only (masked `+256•••••456`, `a•••@domain` otherwise); children shown as first name + class only; payments show reference/amount/status; no query/PII in URLs, logs or audit entries; control, zero-width and bidi characters stripped; min 2 letters / 3 with a digit, max 64 code points (longer is refused, not truncated); results rendered as text nodes + `<mark>` (no HTML); 20 per group (asks for 21, `hasMore` + "See all" link to the list route only if the role can open it, no query in the link); no offset ever (5,000 cap); rate limit hook (30 per 10 s per admin, in-memory, per instance); audit hook `AuditSink` (SEC-6/ADM-6: "all admin actions are audit-logged", so a search that can return personal data is logged: actor, roles, types, counts, query kind, never the query or results; fail closed with 503 if the sink throws; curator catalogue-only searches not logged). Default sink is `UnwiredAuditSink` (persists nothing) until M06 #41 supplies the real one.
- **Dates/phones:** `2026-11-25`, `25/11/2026`, `25-11-2026` become the Africa/Kampala day (UTC range); partial phones (`0772123`, `+256 772`, `(0772) 123`) reduce to a digit fragment matched with `contains` on the stored number.
- **Appwrite adapter (`src/lib/search/appwrite.ts`): UNVERIFIED, mock-tested only.** Acts with the admin's session (`setSession`), no key. Tables used: `user_profile` (+ `parent_profile`/`teacher_profile` to tell parents from teachers), `child`, `session` (+ child/teacher name joins), `payment.provider_reference` and `safeguarding_case` (both M04, not on main when written), `topic.title`, `question.stem`. Open points for staging: query operators/indexes (`contains` on name/phone/email/reference needs checking at 100k sessions, NFR-4), parent/teacher lookups fetch up to 100 user_profile rows then filter by profile (a very common name can crowd out parents; relevance/indexing to tune), SG scope = ids from up to 100 open cases (`child_id`, `teacher_id`, `session_id`; assumes `child.parent_id` -> `parent_profile.$id`), cases have no human reference column so they are found by exact id or category, invoices are not searched (only payments).
- **Commands (worktree, scratch npm cache, one at a time):** `cd apps/admin && npm ci && npx tsc --noEmit && npm test && npm run lint && npm run build` -> tsc clean, 251 passed (was 124+; 1 skipped before a build), lint clean, build clean (route `/api/search` listed, `.next/static` has no API key reference). `make setup`, `make lint` 11 ran ok 2 skipped 0 failed, `make test` 13 ran ok 0 failed; `python3 scripts/secrets_check.py --write` then check: inventory ok (55 entries). No Appwrite contact at all.

### K01 · Catalogue read API with versioning (#50)

- **Status:** **merged** (reviewer round 2, PR #194, squash commit c5af38d, issue #50 closed; real K06 content, staging 3G numbers and real Appwrite `orderAsc`+`cursorAfter` semantics stay owner-blocked, recorded in Follow-ups). Previous status: ready-for-review (round 2).
- **Built (`services/core/src/hs_core/catalogue/`, wired in `main.py` as `create_app(catalogue_service=...)`, `app.state.catalogue`):** `service.py` (`CatalogueService`, read only, takes any `Repository`; `Viewer`; `PUBLIC_STATUSES`, `DRAFT_ROLES = rules.CUR_OPS`), `api.py` (router `/v1/catalogue`), `http.py` (render, strong ETag, gzip, 304), `cache.py` (LRU of rendered immutable content), `__init__.py` (`build_catalogue_service`). Settings (all in `.env.example`, F09 inventory, `config-matrix.md`): `CATALOGUE_IMMUTABLE_MAX_AGE_S` (31536000), `CATALOGUE_CURRENT_MAX_AGE_S` (60), `CATALOGUE_BUNDLE_MAX_BYTES` (1048576), `CATALOGUE_CACHE_ENTRIES` (32).
- **Endpoints (any signed-in user; drafts only `curator`/`opsadmin`, exact labels):** `GET /v1/catalogue/curricula`; `/curricula/{id}/versions` (published+retired; drafts for curator/ops); `/curricula/{id}/current` (the pointer; 404 `no_published_version`; 503 `publish_in_progress` + `Retry-After: 1` inside the retire-then-publish gap); `/versions/{vid}`, `/levels`, `/subjects`, `/strands?subject_id&level_id`, `/topics?subject_id&level_id&parent_id&roots&cursor&limit<=100`, `/topics/search?q&subject_id&level_id&limit<=50`, `/topics/{tid}` (version scoped: a retired version still resolves its topics), `/tree?level_id&subject_id` (nested), `/bundle?level_id&subject_id` (compact `cols`+`rows`), `/level-equivalences` (curator/ops only, as M05); `/struggle-tags`; `/changes?since_version&to_version` (added/removed/retitled, matched by subject+level+topic code; capped 1,000 per list with `truncated`; interface for K04); `/children/{child_id}/version|topics|topics/search|bundle` (the child's PINNED `catalogue_version_id`).
- **Decisions:** (1) unknown id, soft-deleted row, a draft a caller may not see and a child a caller may not read are one identical 404 (no probing); (2) child access = `desired_permissions("child", row, ctx)` of M05 (`RepoFacts`/`PermissionContext`): parent, guardian with grant `children`, teacher while assigned/substitute window, ops, assigned safeguarding lead; not duplicated; (3) "new logs use the child's current version" is read as: the version pinned on the child, even when retired, until a repin (P03/K-ticket); `/children/{id}/version` also reports `is_current` and `current_version_id`; (4) ETag = `"<version id>.<sha256(body)[:32]>"`, the gzip representation has its own `-gz` tag; published/retired content `private, max-age=<1 year>, immutable`; pointers `private, max-age=60`; drafts and per-child answers `private, no-cache` (the permission check runs BEFORE a 304); `private` because requests carry Authorization; (5) cursors are opaque, bound to (version, filters) and the cursor row must belong to that version (400 `invalid_cursor` otherwise); keyset on (sort_order, id), never offset, so pages over a 5,300-topic version work; soft-deleted rows are skipped by in-memory filtering (Appwrite has no isNull in the fake); (6) search = in-process index per immutable version (LRU), every word must match code/title/description case-insensitively, rank code, title prefix, title, description; (7) hard cap on tree/bundle (`CATALOGUE_BUNDLE_MAX_BYTES`, 413 `response_too_large`, page through `/topics` instead); (8) level equivalences stay curator/ops (M05 withholds the table from apps); if matching (X/Q tickets) needs them for teachers that is an M05 decision; (9) the write rules of `appwrite/catalogue_rules.py` (publish/retire/copy) are K02-K04 and are NOT used here; this module relies on their invariants (immutable published versions, one published per curriculum, retire first then publish).
- **Measured (offline, in-memory repo, 20-hex ids):** bundle of a 2,000-topic subject 290,031 B raw (145 B/topic), 59,186 B gzip, cold build 157 ms, warm well under 300 ms; search over 3,000 topics worst warm 8 ms; cold search on seed-sized data well under 300 ms. Real Appwrite adds ~1 request per 500 rows on the first (cold) build of a version per instance; warm-up job is a follow-up. NOT measured: staging on 3G.
- **Test command:** `cd services/core && uv run pytest -q tests/test_catalogue_*.py` (api 81, scale 7, concurrency 2, mutations 21 tests; ~12 s); full core run `uv run pytest -q`. Pre-existing time-of-day flake not caused by K01: `tests/test_notify_misc.py::test_app_binds_the_dispatch_job_and_wires_no_real_channels` fails when run during quiet hours (push is `deferred`, not `failed`); same failure on main.

- **Reviewer round 1 (2026-11-26, 849f13b on main 448df8d): changes-requested, one blocking finding.**
  - *Evidence (fresh worktree):* `cd services/core && uv sync && uv run pytest -q` 2368 passed, 1 failed = the known `test_notify_misc.py::test_app_binds_the_dispatch_job_and_wires_no_real_channels` (identical failure on clean origin/main 448df8d, run at 00:24 EAT, quiet hours); `-k catalogue` 130 passed x3; ruff check/format clean; `secrets_check.py` ok and `--write` no diff; `make setup`, `make lint` exit 0 (11 ran ok); `make test` = 11 ok, 2 failed: core (the same clock test) and nested `scripts` (its layout test runs `make test`, so it fails only because core fails); `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` all passed. Diff = K01 only (15 files), no secrets, no Appwrite calls.
  - *BLOCKING: `GET /v1/catalogue/versions/{version_id}` is served `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` but its body holds mutable fields (`status`, `retired_at`).* Reproduced: fetch v2 (published, immutable 1 year), publish v3, fetch v2 again: now `retired`. A client that cached the first answer believes v2 is `published` for a year and, because of `immutable`, never revalidates; a header shipped to the field cannot be recalled. Content endpoints (levels, subjects, strands, topics, tree, bundle, search, changes between two fixed versions) are fine: rows of a published/retired version never change (`catalogue_rules.PROTECTED_FIELDS` blocks edits, soft delete included). Fix: `/versions/{id}` (and anything else that embeds `p_version`) must use the `pointer` or `revalidate` kind, never `immutable`; add a test (fetch published, publish the next version, status changes, `Cache-Control` not `immutable`) and a mutant for it. `children/{id}/version`, `current`, `versions` list are already pointer/no-cache: unaffected.
  - *Non-blocking (record, fix when convenient):* (a) mutant "drop `Vary: Authorization`" survives the suite (`private` already protects; add an assertion); (b) the `503 publish_in_progress` carries no `Cache-Control`: add `no-store`; Flutter follow-up: offline-first apps must treat 503 + `Retry-After` as "keep the cached bundle and retry", and 404 `no_published_version` as "nothing to show yet"; (c) a child that exists but is unreadable is distinguishable from an unknown id by about 1 ms (in-memory fake: 7.1 vs 6.0 ms median over 1,000 samples; status, body shape and headers identical): negligible with random ids, but the real adapter makes more calls for an existing child; (d) mutant "cursor not bound to version" survives because the cursor-row-belongs-to-version check also refuses it (equivalent mutant, fine).
  - *Attacked and held:* role matrix over 17 token variants x 13 draft/child paths (drafts and draft pins only for exact `curator`/`opsadmin`; `Curator`, `cura_tor`, `OPSADMIN`, `ops_admin`, superadmin, lead, parent, guardian, teachers all 404); unknown id, soft-deleted version, hidden draft, other family's child, ended teacher, curator on a child, unpinned child, draft pin: same status, body shape and headers; child endpoints `private, no-cache` + `Vary: Accept-Encoding, Authorization`, and follow M05 (mutated `SPECS["child"]` to nobody: parent 404; to another uid: that uid 200); level-equivalences curator/ops only; interleaved curator/parent requests on a draft never leak (a draft is never in the LRU, public only after publish); 304 for exact/weak/multiple/`*`, 200 for malformed/unquoted/empty/other; gzip has its own `-gz` tag; a title change flips the ETag; cursors forged/other-version/other-row/deleted-row/garbage/5,000 chars/non-string/other filters all 400 or 422, limits 0/-1/101/huge/abc 422; search with `%`, `_`, `.*`, `(a+)+$`, NUL, bidi, SQL-ish, 80 chars: no error, no ReDoS (plain substring), cannot reach draft or other-version topics; tree/bundle of a 2,000-topic subject 398 KB, 6 repository list calls cold and 0 warm, search warm 0 calls; 40 distinct queries do not evict the topic index; publish gap 503 + `Retry-After: 1`, 404 after 10 s; two published rows resolve to the newest in `current` and `curricula`. Mutants killed: shared-cacheable child response, `public` Cache-Control, drafts immutable, drafts to parents, soft delete ignored, constant ETag, cross-family child, mixed versions, topic cache keyed without version, child uses current version.

### M05 · Row and file permissions for all roles (Appwrite) (#40)

- **Status:** blocked-foundation-merged (PR #192, squash 80093f3, 2026-11-25; reviewer round 2 approved). Owner-blocked: throwaway-project verification of the fake's assumptions, owner questions 28-35, live drift run, label-directory wiring (staging blocker). Earlier: ready-for-review (round 2) · branch `m05-permissions` (one commit `M05:` `0458db5`, rebased on main 43ca820, pushed --force-with-lease; PR not opened); was changes-requested (reviewer round 1) · was ready-for-review. Owner-blocked parts as below; expect `blocked-foundation-merged`.
- **Built (`services/core/src/hs_core/permissions/`):** `rules.py` (`desired_permissions(table, row, ctx)` and `desired_file_permissions(bucket, owner_row, ctx)`; ONE `Spec` per table in `SPECS`: 68 schema tables + 5 pending service-only decisions for tables of open tickets), `context.py` (`PermissionContext` over a `FactSource` = `StaticFacts` or `RepoFacts`: assignments, substitute windows, 24 h address rule, cases, guardians, account status, labels; all UTC), `policy.py` (labels, `Policy` switches, constants), `strings.py` (build/parse/forbidden), `service.py` (`PermissionService`: events -> recompute affected scopes -> write only the diff; `run(event, business_write)` = ADR 0012 order; `RetryQueue` on the F04 runner; `Seen`, `ApplyReport`, `PartialApplication`), `jobs.py` (`address-revoke`, `permission-reconcile`, `permission-audit`; `bind`), `audit.py` (`Auditor`, `audit_all`, chunked and resumable, report only), `fake.py` (permission-aware fake Appwrite: `PermissionAwareRepository.as_principal(Principal)` with `read(...)` evaluation, rowSecurity, table permissions, cursor paging, `$updatedAt`; `FakeFileStore`), `files.py` (`AppwriteFileStore`, mock-tested only), `history.py` (`build_history_row`), `matrixdoc.py` (generated docs), `sample.py` (seeded dataset, one+ row in every table), `wiring.py` (binds the jobs in `create_app`), `queries.py` (JSON query strings + cursor paging, no SDK import). Schedule: three `JobSpec`s in `jobs/schedule.py`. Script `services/core/scripts/permissions_check.py` (drift check; `--write-docs`; `--live` read-only audit, never run). Docs `docs/ops/permissions.md` (matrix generated from code). Schema: NEW domain `appwrite/schema/permissions.json` (`access_window`, `teacher_session_history`), migration `0008_permissions_schema.py` (0006 A01, 0007 X01; renumber if another ticket merges first), ERD and `appwrite.config.json` regenerated. `packages.json`: core lint also runs the drift script. No new env settings, so nothing for `.env.example` or the F09 inventory (policy switches are code constants in `policy.py`, one line each).
- **Design decisions (read these):**
  1. *Order of operations = ADR 0012:* enqueue `permission-reconcile` (dedupe `permission-reconcile:event:<id>`, run_at +60 s) -> business write -> recompute and write permissions. "Revoke before or atomically" is not possible in Appwrite; revokes follow the same path, a failed revoke is an over-grant until the job retries, alerts `permission_over_grant` from the first job attempt, and the access window stays open so `address-revoke` retries too.
  2. *Computed from state, never add/remove one entry:* the whole list is replaced (`update(table, id, {}, permissions=[...])`), so twice = no-op and the append-only tables (audit_log, safeguarding_action, ledger_*) only have their list rewritten (columns untouched, tested).
  3. *`access_window`:* discovery index for time lapses (one row per teacher+child with the next lapse instant, `by_due (state, expires_at)`), because Appwrite cannot query "assignments that ended more than 24 h ago" without an index on `end_at`/`scheduled_end_at` (existing indexes lead with other columns). It is only an optimisation; permissions always come from state and the nightly reconcile/audit repair a missing row. Windows advance only after a CLEAN run (bug found by a test: a later scope skipping an already-failed row closed the window; fixed with `Seen.failed`).
  4. *Teacher access:* needs profile `active`, account `active`, label `teacher`. Current or upcoming assignment (`end_at` empty or future) = child, logs, handover, topic progress, address. Ended: child data gone at once; address stays until last HELD session end + 24 h unless `end_reason` in `parent_cancelled|admin|teacher_withdrew|rematch_requested` (immediate). Substitute (`session.is_substitute`): child, logs, address, handover, own session row from the session row's existence until covered session end + 24 h; cancelled/void = at once. Own account rows (earnings, documents, history, profile) stay with a suspended teacher; a deleted user is on no row.
  5. *Anonymised history* = new table `teacher_session_history` (first name, class, Kampala date, duration; `read(user:<teacher>)` + ops), as ADR 0012 says; `history.build_history_row` is for the log-submit ticket (L02/L03) to call.
  6. *Safeguarding lead:* per-row `read(user:<assigned lead>)` (M04 schema note) on the case, its actions and `case_status`; related rows (child, family address, session logs, teacher documents) only while an OPEN case concerns them and the lead is assigned; a closed case withdraws those rows and leaves the case record with the lead (`Policy.lead_keeps_closed_case_record`). Unassigned cases are invisible to every client (`Policy.unassigned_cases_visible_to_leads=False`): the admin queue/assignment goes through a core endpoint. Ops admin has no read on cases. `audit_log`: row-level `read("label:opsadmin")` and `read("label:safeguardinglead")` (flag `Policy.audit_log_lead_read`, Q12 default as recorded).
  7. *Super admin:* only `role_assignment` and `notification_template` (SR 6.1 role management; Q16a default kept: not implicit).
  8. *Guardian scopes:* `guardian_link.grants` (the column is `grants`, not `permissions`) is a comma list of `children, logs, sessions, billing, address, consents` or `all`; empty/unknown = nothing; link must be `active`, guardian holds label `guardian`.
  9. *No client write anywhere:* `CLIENT_WRITABLE_TABLES = frozenset()`; `WRITE_PROTECTED` lists money, tier, earning, payout, session status, check-in validity (+ safeguarding, audit, consent). Reference tables a client cannot read are served by core endpoints.
  10. *Reconcile never writes `$updatedAt`-free:* it scans business tables by `$updatedAt >= scheduled_for - 48 h` (own permission writes bump it once: harmless) plus overdue open windows; older drift is found by the audit, not repaired by reconcile (test `test_nightly_reconcile_looks_back_only...`).
- **Discrepancies in ADR 0012 / SR (ADRs NOT edited; for the decision owner):** (a) ADR table says curators get `create/update/delete` via `label:curator` and `notification_preference` is user-writable (`update("user:<id>")`); M03/M04 and the ticket route all of it through the core service, implemented as zero client writes (change `CLIENT_WRITABLE_TABLES` + rules if the owner wants it back). (b) ADR table says published `question` rows are `read("users")`; implemented as curator + ops only because `answer_key` sits in the row and SR 6.2 lets parents/teachers see published TOPICS only; media files of an approved question are `read("users")`. (c) ADR says the daily audit also repairs; the ticket says no writes: audit reports, reconcile repairs. (d) ADR `safeguarding_case` is `read("label:safeguardinglead")`; implemented per-row assigned lead (M04 note, scope rule). (e) SR matrix "Own account: ops -" vs ADR `user_profile` row ops read: followed the ADR. (f) SR matrix lead "Read" for session logs and teacher documents is blanket; followed the ADR scope rule (open case only). (g) ADR names tables `home_address`, `teacher_session_history`, `handover_summary`; schema names are `home_location`, (new) `teacher_session_history`, `handover_summary`. (h) ADR 0002 job table still lacks `permission-reconcile` and `permission-audit` (and `address-revoke` wording): add in the next ADR PR.
- **Honest process note:** the rules and service were written BEFORE their tests this time (not red-first). To compensate: tests map every criterion below, and I ran 12+ mutations (grace removed, substitute grace 0, closed case ignored, label requirement dropped, guardian grants ignored, `users` on child, revoked token readable, window closed on failed revoke, address-revoke made a no-op, ...): each is killed by at least one test. The failed-revoke/window bug was found by a test, not by design.
- **Test commands and results (real, rebased tree):** `cd services/core && uv sync && uv run pytest -q` -> **1756 passed** (778 of them in `tests/test_perm_*.py`: strings, rules, 559 matrix cases, service, jobs, property, files, history, schema/docs); `uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check .` clean; `uv run python scripts/permissions_check.py` -> `permissions ok: zero differences` (168 rows, 6 files audited); `cd appwrite && python3 -m unittest discover -s tests` Ran 404 OK (new `tests/test_permissions_schema.py`, 11); `python3 appwrite/schema_build.py --check` -> schema ok (71 tables); `python3 scripts/secrets_check.py`, `scripts/check_layout.py`, `scripts/secret_scan.py`, `docs/adr/check-adrs.py` ok. NOT run: `make lint/test` as a whole (admin needs `npm ci`), `scripts/tests`, pre-commit.

- **Owner-blocked (exact steps):** need a throwaway Appwrite project (F02 `hs-staging` or a local one), a scoped API key, and the core service pointed at it. 1) Apply migrations through `0008` (`python3 appwrite/migrate.py --env staging`). 2) Create one user per role via `users.create` + `users.updateLabels` (confirm that `opsadmin` is accepted and `ops_admin` is rejected: ADR 0012 assumption 1). 3) Create rows with `permissions=['read("user:<id>")']` through the core service's `AppwriteRepository`; sign in as each user (account.createSession) and list/get with the client SDK: confirm exactly the allowed rows come back, an unreadable `get` is 404/401, no `create/update/delete` works (assumptions 2, 3 in `docs/ops/permissions.md`). 4) Confirm `update_row(data={}, permissions=[...])` replaces `$permissions` without touching columns and that a Storage `update_file(permissions=...)` does the same (assumption 3). 5) Confirm `$updatedAt` and `cursorAfter` queries work without extra indexes (assumption 5). 6) Run `cd services/core && uv run python scripts/permissions_check.py --live` (read only) after `PermissionService` rebuilt each table once (`Scope("table", t)`): expect zero differences. 7) Q12 and Q16a answers (below) change one line in `policy.py` / the matrix each.

(2) Whatever sets/removes labels (A01 does it at sign-up; the admin role-management ticket A-51) must send `role_changed`. (3) L02/L03 (log submit) calls `history.build_history_row`. (4) Labels today come from `role_assignment` history (`RoleAssignmentLabels`); wire the A01 Appwrite directory (authoritative labels) behind the `LabelDirectory` protocol before staging. (5) The `access_window`/`teacher_session_history` tables and `permissions` domain need the live-diff note: they do not exist live. (6) The reconcile scans use `$updatedAt` and `cursorAfter`: verify on a real project (step 5). (7) `job_run`/`job_attempt` (F04 follow-up 6) and any future table need a `Spec` or service-only entry: the matrix test fails otherwise (pending decisions for `otp_challenge`, `travel_time_cache`, `travel_usage`, `job_run`, `job_attempt` are already in `PENDING_SERVICE_ONLY`, then become normal service-only entries). (8) ADR 0012 follow-up PR (not here): update the table text per the discrepancy list and the ADR 0002 job table.

(b) The event path writes inline without a lock, so a stale concurrent recompute can re-grant a just-revoked entry until that event's retry job (+60 s) or the nightly reconcile; ADR 0012 accepts last-writer-wins with recompute-from-state, the exposure is bounded by the job, say so in the docs. ### A03 · Role model and app routing (#45)

- **Status:** **ready-for-review (round 3: M06 integrated)** (branch head `6b4e2ae`, ONE commit `A03:` squashed on main c7b0cb5+; pushed --force-with-lease; PR not opened; see "Builder round 3 (A03)" below). Previous: ready-for-review (round 2), changes-requested (reviewer round 1)
- **Honest process note:** the model, service and reconcile code were written in the same pass as their tests (not red-first); to compensate I ran mutation checks: 15 Python mutations (self-grant check, ops grants staff, blocked target, last-superadmin guard and its race net, grant lock, claim conflict, audit skipped, bare-string labels, inactive roles, route table, Q27, suspended profile, staff grant completion, unguarded endpoint) and 12 Dart mutations (guards, parent admits teacher, unknown labels, resume refresh, stale refresh, offline cache, open redirect): all killed except two equivalent Python mutants (a bare string iterates as characters that are never labels; the endpoint guard duplicates the service check). Writing the mutation runs found three weak tests, now strengthened (last-superadmin refusal before any write, lock serialisation, stale-read claim).
- **Built, core (`services/core/src/hs_core/roles/`):** `model.py` (ROLES, STAFF_ROLES, APP_ROLES, ADMIN_ROUTES; pure `clean_labels`, `roles_for`, `app_admits`, `admin_admits`, `app_decision`), `service.py` (`RoleService.grant_role/revoke_role`, `Actor`, policy, errors), `audit.py` (`RoleAuditSink`, fake, interim `RepositoryRoleSink` -> `audit_log`), `directory.py` (users protocol), `reconcile.py` (`role-reconcile` job + `reconcile_chunk`), `api.py` (`GET /v1/me/access?app=`, `POST /v1/admin/roles/{id}/grant|revoke`), `labels.py` (M05 `LabelDirectory` over Appwrite, NOT wired by default), `factory.py`. Changed: `auth.py` (labels via `clean_labels`), `auth_otp/accounts.py` (Q27, suspended, revoked, grants via the role service; `UserDirectory` gained `get`, `remove_label`, `labelled`; `AppwriteUserDirectory` implements them, mock-tested), `auth_otp/service.py` (errors `staff_account`, `account_suspended`, `role_revoked`), `main.py` (one shared user directory, `app.state.roles`, routers, job binding), `jobs/schedule.py` (`role-reconcile` hourly), `settings.py` (+2), `permissions/wiring.py` (optional `labels` argument). Docs `docs/ops/roles.md`, `docs/ops/roles-matrix.json` (shared fixture), core README.
- **Design decisions (defaults, owner questions 42-47):** grant order = audit intent, history row, label, `role_changed` (row first: a crash leaves the user with LESS than recorded; a revoke closes the row first and the label call is retried by the caller and by `role-reconcile`). Active-grant uniqueness = claim row id from (user, role, generation) because the fake and Appwrite have no conditional update; per-user lock in process. Staff grants: superadmin only, never self, system never. `parent/guardian/teacher`: ops or superadmin or the system. Last superadmin is protected (checked before and after, for the two-revokers race). Staff label without a row: report only. A role staff withdrew is not given back by signing in.
- **Flutter (`packages/flutter_shared`):** `auth/roles.dart` (labels, `AppId`, `decideApp`), `auth/session_controller.dart` (`sessionProvider`, `SessionController.refresh/signOut`, `AccessSource` hook), `nav/route_guard.dart` (pure `guardRedirect`, `safeFrom`, `SessionGate`), `nav/app_router.dart` (guarded routes, `landingPath` and `tabBuilder` hooks; `AppSpec.id`), `widgets/access_screens.dart` (`StartScreen`, `WrongAppScreen`, `SuspendedScreen`), `HsApp` (starts and syncs the session), `SessionRefresher` (refresh = re-evaluate roles), `AuthSession.labels/status`, `StoredSession.labels/status` (offline start), `SessionGateway.account()`, OTP failures `suspended`, `staffAccount`, `roleRevoked`. Apps: `main.dart` no longer picks a start screen (the router restores the session behind a splash).
- **Admin:** `apps/admin/tests/roles-fixture.test.ts` (reads the shared fixture). `roles.ts`/`nav.ts` unchanged (they already match).

- **Deviations / honest limits:** (1) `AppSpec` now has a required `id` (V01 also edits `AppSpec`: expect a trivial merge conflict; V01's `postSignInPath` should be passed as `buildAppRouter(landingPath: (s) => spec.postSignInPath)`; V03 gates it by status). (2) In-session suspension needs a core-backed `AccessSource` (V03; needs V01's bearer-token plumbing): today suspension reaches the app through the sign-in (`account_suspended`), a Appwrite-blocked account (session ends) and the `AccessSource` hook. (3) The apps learn labels from `account.get()` (UI only; the server never trusts it). (4) Existing A01/shared tests were changed because routes are now guarded: tests opening a tab sign in with `FakeAuthService.signInAs`; the old A01 staff test was replaced by the Q27 tests; the "new flow after sign-out" test now actually signs out. (5) M06 is not merged: `RepositoryRoleSink` is the interim sink; after M06 merges, `RoleService` must write `role_assignment` through an `AuditContext` (M06 registry lists the table) and a real `AuditedRoleSink` replaces the interim one. (6) `DirectoryLabels` exists but M05 still derives labels from `role_assignment` (identical while this service keeps both in step): switch when the owner has verified the directory. (7) The N01 wall-clock test above fails between 21:00 and 07:00 Kampala time: not touched here.

### clock-matrix · chore: time-of-day dependent tests (no ticket)

Status: merged. PR #196 squash-merged as 42184af (commit 68f9e0d on branch). Reproduced at night locally; fixed.
- **Fix:** `build_notification_service(..., clock=None)` (production default unchanged: real clock); the test is split into `test_app_binds_the_dispatch_job`, `test_wires_no_real_channels_by_day` (push failed) and `test_wires_no_real_channels_at_night_push_is_deferred` (push deferred).

The runner names every test that fails at some clocks only. Results: core (2256 tests) x 16 clocks, py-momo, appwrite, legal-docs, ops x 16 clocks, scripts x 2 clocks (night + day; it nests make test): the ONLY clock-dependent test was the one above (verified: with the old test the matrix reports it failing at wed-23:30 only). No production Kampala-vs-UTC-date bug found.

The core CI job gets a step: `scripts/clock_matrix.py --targets core --clocks quick --select 'tests/test_notify*.py,tests/test_perm*.py,tests/test_jobs*.py,tests/test_ledger*.py'` (2 clocks: wed-23:30 and month-end-23:30; measured 2.5 min locally on a busy machine for both clocks).
- **Docs:** CONTRIBUTING.md "Tests must not read the wall clock", CLAUDE.md rule 11. Unit tests: `scripts/tests/test_clock_matrix.py`.
- **Commands:** `python3 scripts/clock_matrix.py --list`; `python3 scripts/clock_matrix.py --targets core --clocks quick`; `--targets core,py-momo,appwrite,legal-docs,ops,scripts`; `--clocks wed-23:30,leap-mar1-00:30`; `--select 'tests/test_notify*.py'`. The frozen clock stands still by default (`--tick` lets it advance).
- **Results at night (real clock 00:45-01:00 Kampala):** core pytest 2256 passed; scripts unittest 195 OK; `make setup && make lint && make test` green (13 ran ok, 0 failed); ruff, actionlint, ci_lint and `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` pass.

- **Reviewer round 1 (A03) — changes-requested.** Verified in a fresh worktree at 6abc998: core `uv run pytest -q` 2514 passed + 1 failed (`test_notify_misc::test_app_binds_the_dispatch_job...`, run at 00:48 and 01:10 EAT; the SAME single test fails identically on clean origin/main d6ff7cf at 01:10 EAT: quiet-hours wall clock, chore `chore-clock-dependent-tests`); ruff check/format clean; `permissions_check.py` zero differences; `secrets_check.py` ok and `--write` no diff; admin `npm ci`, `tsc`, `npm test` 256 passed (1 skipped), lint, build ok; flutter_shared analyze/format clean, 264 tests passed; parent and teacher analyze/format clean, 9 tests each passed; `make setup && make lint` 11 ok 2 skipped 0 failed; `make test` 11 ok 2 failed (both the same wall-clock test: once in `services/core`, once via nested `scripts` `test_make_test_and_lint_exit_zero`); `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` all passed.
