Mutations: 19 new Python mutants (system grants staff, ops grants staff, self-grant, role claims hard-coded in `_actor`, Q27 removed or narrowed, labels case-insensitive, parent app admits teacher, status ignored, audit intent skipped, suspended target allowed, last-superadmin guard off, revoke leaves label, staff-withdrawn regrant, revoke authz open, reconcile revoked-row removal off, reconcile backfills staff, unknown role accepted, `/me/access` any app): 18 killed, 1 survivor (`require_roles` on the endpoints: equivalent, the service re-checks); 7 Dart mutants (guard-by-default off, splash lets protected through, wrong-app admitted, suspended admitted, sign-out keeps state: killed; `safeFrom` without the `//` or scheme checks: survive but equivalent, `hasAuthority`/leading-`/` rules still reject). Shared fixture mutated (route role: Python + admin fail; `apps.parent` role: Python + Dart fail; the admin TS has no mobile table and Dart has no admin routes, so each side only checks what it has). A01 tests changed by the builder (old staff test replaced by Q27 tests, sign-out test now signs out, tab tests sign in): no weakening found. Only `RoleService` and the directory call `add_label`/`remove_label`; no env/setting grants superadmin. Q27: refusal is after `claim_consume`, so after the code is proven; label decides (a staff `role_assignment` row without the label does not block sign-in and gives no staff access; reconcile closes such a row). M06 is still open (#193): `RepositoryRoleSink` is still the interim writer; a tripwire check is not possible until M06 merges.
  - **Finding 1 (must fix): `role-reconcile` is not isolated against a malformed row.** `reconcile._row_without_label` does `row["user_id"]` and `parse(row["granted_at"])` outside any handler, and `_label_without_row` the same for rows from `svc._rows`: a `role_assignment` row missing `user_id` or `granted_at`, or with an unparseable `granted_at`, raises KeyError/ValueError out of `reconcile_chunk` (reproduced: `KeyError: 'granted_at'`, `ValueError: Invalid isoformat string: 'garbage'`). The job then fails, retries the same cursor and never advances, so every later drift repair stops, and this job is the only backstop for the revoke window below. The ticket brief and module docstring promise per-row isolation. Fix: catch `Exception` per row (log row id, count as `failed`, continue) and add tests for rows missing `user_id`/`granted_at`/bad timestamp that assert the rest of the page is still repaired.
  - **Finding 2 (must fix, small): staff revoke leaves the staff label on a crash/failed label call.** `_revoke` closes the history row first and removes the label second. If the label call fails (Appwrite 5xx: the likeliest partial failure) or the process dies between the two writes (reproduced with crash injection: row closed, label `opsadmin` still set), the user keeps working admin/core access (core guards read the label from the live `account.get()`, so access ends the moment the label goes, not at JWT expiry) while the history says revoked. The caller gets 503 `role_change_incomplete` and can repeat; otherwise the hourly `role-reconcile` removes it (up to ~1 h, plus F04 retries). For a staff role that is the wrong way round: revoke must remove ACCESS first. Fix: for `STAFF_ROLES` remove the label first, then close the row (reconcile already closes a staff row that has no label, and a repeat of the call finishes it); keep row-first for non-staff (label-first there would let reconcile re-grant the label). Add the crash-after-every-write test for a staff revoke asserting the user is never left with the label and a closed row.
  - **Non-blocking notes (no change required):** (a) multi-instance: the per-user lock is in-process and `AppwriteUserDirectory.add_label/remove_label` read-modify-write the whole label list, so two instances changing labels of one user within milliseconds can lose an update (e.g. resurrect a just-revoked label); the claim row keeps rows right and `role-reconcile` heals it (revoked row + label -> remove). Acceptable; worth an owner line when scaling beyond one Fly machine. (b) `_is_last_superadmin` counts active ROWS only: an owner-bootstrapped label-only superadmin is not counted, so a row-based superadmin cannot be revoked by that bootstrap admin ("last_superadmin"); conservative, never over-grants. (c) No rate limit on `/v1/admin/roles/*` (JWT + opsadmin/superadmin required; role names are a closed 7-value enum; refusals are audited): fine. (d) A staff label added between the Q27 check and token issue yields a mobile token for that user for one sign-in: no staff access gained (admin needs MFA, A04). (e) An in-flight `refreshSession` that began before sign-out can re-save the (already deleted) session secret and cached labels into the vault after `signOut` cleared it (generation guard protects only provider state): harmless (server session deleted, labels are UI-only, next start gets 401 and clears) but could be tightened with V01's sign-out work. (f) `admin_admits` treats `/payments/%2e%2e/settings` as `/payments/...` (no percent-decoding): matches the TS guard; Next does not decode that segment into a different route. (g) Owner-blocked items and questions 42-47 defaults recorded and acceptable.
  - **After the fixes:** rebase onto main (V01 also edits `AppSpec`; union-resolve secrets/schedule/main.py; run `python3 scripts/secrets_check.py --write`), rerun the three suites; merge needs a green PR `ci-ok` (the wall-clock test is only acceptable once the clock chore has merged).
  - **Builder round 2 (A03).** Finding 1: `reconcile._isolated` wraps every row (`_row_without_label`, `_label_without_row`): `Exception` (not BaseException) is caught per row, logged by table + row id only (error type, no values), counted `malformed`/`failed`, alerted (`role_malformed_row`), and the cursor advances; repository/directory outages are NOT swallowed (the chunk fails and F04 retries). `user_id` must be a 1-36 char string; `granted_at` must parse; `RoleService._rows` sorts timestamps as strings so a wrong-typed history row cannot break a change for that user. Tests (`test_roles_reconcile.py`): 12 malformed shapes x (rest of the page repaired, label phase), unknown role ignored, unknown user closed, log has the row id only and the alert fires, outages still abort, cursor advances with small pages, a 10,000-row run with 500 malformed (20 repaired, 500 counted, ~25 s). Finding 2: a STAFF revoke is now audit intent, label removed, `UserDirectory.end_sessions` (best effort; `users.delete_sessions`, mock-tested, real check owner-blocked), history row closed, event; non-staff stays row-first; staff grant stays row-first. `test_crash_before_every_write_of_a_staff_revoke_never_leaves_label_and_closed_row` (3 writes), reconcile closes the left-over staff row, failing label call leaves everything unchanged and a repeat finishes, session-end failure is not fatal, mobile revoke ends no sessions. `Boom` in the test helper is now a BaseException (a dead process runs no handlers). Non-blocking: `docs/ops/roles.md` "Limits" (multi-instance lock and label read-modify-write healed by reconcile, last superadmin counts rows only); per-actor rate limit on `/v1/admin/roles/*` (`ROLE_CHANGES_PER_HOUR`, default 60, 429 + Retry-After, in `.env.example` and the F09 inventory, 88 entries); an in-flight refresh can no longer re-save the session secret/cached labels after sign-out (generation counter in `AppwriteAuthService`, 2 tests) and the provider-level stale refresh has a test. M06 is NOT merged at the final rebase (origin/main has no M06 commit): follow-up for whoever merges second: `RoleService` must write `role_assignment` through an `AuditContext`-bound `AuditedRepository` (actor from the verified principal or the system name), `RepositoryRoleSink` becomes an `AuditedRoleSink`, and M06's tripwire test must be run on the branch. Results: core `uv run pytest -q` 2554 passed + 1 failed = the N01 wall-clock test, proven identical on a clean origin/main worktree at 01:25 EAT (`{'push': 'deferred'} != {'push': 'failed'}`); ruff check/format clean; `permissions_check.py` zero differences; `secrets_check.py` ok (88) and `--write` no diff; flutter_shared 267 passed, parent 9, teacher 9, analyze/format clean in all three; admin `npm ci`, tsc, 256 passed (1 skipped), lint, build ok; `make setup && make lint` 11 ok 2 skipped 0 failed; `make test` 11 ok 2 failed (the same N01 test, directly and through nested `test_make_test_and_lint_exit_zero`; the run also caught my own new test failing against the fake's mixed-type sort, fixed afterwards and core rerun green apart from N01); `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` 12 passed. Mutations rerun: Python 8 new (staff revoke label-after-row, no session end, reconcile without isolation, outages swallowed, cursor not advancing, malformed not counted, rate limit off, unsafe sort) all killed after adding two tests (small-page cursor, wrong-typed timestamp); Dart 5 (in-flight refresh re-save, guards, resume refresh, stale refresh publishes) all killed after adding two race tests; the round-1 lists were not re-run in full.
  - **Builder round 3 (A03): M06 integrated.** M06 (#193), K01 (#194), M05 and the clock chore are on main; the branch was squashed to one commit and rebased (conflicts in `accounts.py`, `schedule.py`, `main.py`, `settings.py`, `.env.example`, README, F09 inventory union-resolved: both sides kept; inventory re-generated, 97 entries). The M06 tripwire (`test_audit_reads.py::TestUnauditedWriteTripwire`) passes on the real tree (26 tests) and A03 adds `test_no_role_or_profile_write_bypasses_the_audited_repository` (runs `find_unaudited_writes` over `hs_core`). **Design:** every `role_assignment` write in `RoleService` (grant claim row, revoke close, superadmin restore, reconcile backfill) goes through `AuditedRepository` with an `AuditContext` (request: `Depends(audit_context)`, actor from the verified principal, key from the SERVER request key; job: `job_scope` = actor `system` + job `role-reconcile`; sign-up: attributed to the new user via `AuditContext.for_principal`, as M06 did). The interim `roles/audit.py` (`RoleAuditSink`, `RepositoryRoleSink`, fake) is DELETED: ONE audit row per role change, M06 intent protocol (audit-first; ambiguous errors left for `audit-recover`). The label is not a table write, so M06's `AuditedRepository.create/update` gained one optional kwarg `before_apply` (runs inside the audited change after the intent row and before the write; `IncompleteChange` is a `NotWritten`, so a failing label call marks the intent `failed` at once); a staff revoke uses it so the label is removed (and sessions ended) first, still inside one audited change, then the row is closed. A label-only repair or revoke is a single `record` row; refusals write one `role.<kind>_refused` row. A01's `_record_grant` is gone (sign-up calls `grant_role`); M06's `_ensure_profile` audit is kept. Registry: `role.grant_staff` and `role.revoke_staff` are in `REASON_REQUIRED` (parent/guardian/teacher changes use `role.grant`/`role.revoke`, no reason); `docs/ops/audit.md` updated ("Role changes"). **Tests (all offline):** `roles_helpers.Env` now builds the service over `AuditedRepository(CountingRepo(raw))` (`e.audit` reads the raw audit rows); exactly one audit row per grant/revoke and a replay with the same request key still one row (`test_a_replay_of_the_same_request_writes_one_audit_row_and_one_role_row`); crash injection after every write (intent, row, label: 3 for a grant incl. staff, 3 for a non-staff revoke, 4 for a staff revoke) with M06 `recover_audit` (`not_applied` for a grant whose row never landed, nothing for one that landed, staff revoke k=2/3/4 settles `not_applied` and a repeat converges, one intent per attempt); audit store down = no label change and no role row (service and HTTP 503, also a staff revoke keeps label, row, sessions); reasons: zero-width / blank / 2-char reasons rejected for staff grants and revokes (service and HTTP 422 `reason_required`), mobile changes need none, registry check; actor from the verified token never the body/headers (`X-Actor-Id`, body `actor_id` = 422); no audit context = `AuditContextMissing` before anything is written; reconcile repairs audited as `system` + job `role-reconcile`; sign-up grant audited as the signing-up user. **Results:** core `uv run pytest -q` 3274 passed 0 failed (night run too: the clock chore is merged); ruff check/format clean; `permissions_check.py` zero differences; `secrets_check.py` ok (97) and `--write` no diff; flutter_shared 267, parent 9, teacher 9 passed, analyze/format clean (rebase did not touch Dart); admin `npm ci`, tsc, 307 tests passed (1 skipped), lint, build ok; `make setup && make lint` 11 ok 2 skipped 0 failed; `make test` 13 ran ok 0 failed (before the final K01 rebase; after it core was rerun 3274 passed); `uvx pre-commit run --all-files` 12 passed. **Mutations re-run:** role write bypassing the audited repo (killed), staff label step dropped (killed), second audit row per change (killed), audit context made optional (killed), API actor not from the principal (killed), registry drops staff reasons (killed); `_need_reason` removed survives and is equivalent (the audit layer enforces the same reason check). The round-2 mutation lists were not re-run in full. Known follow-up: the A01 phone flow writes `user_profile` through M06's `bind_actor` (bare repositories in unit tests are not audited, the app's are).

## Questions for the owner (collected; ask at the end)

- 2026-11-25: H09 global search. Please confirm guesses: (a) ops sees phone/email unmasked in results, safeguarding lead masked; (b) ops does NOT search the catalogue or safeguarding cases (SR 6.2 allows Read on the catalogue, top-bar spec lists people/sessions/payments only); (c) safeguarding lead searches teachers/parents/children/sessions only when linked to an open case; (d) every search that can return personal data is audit-logged (SEC-6/ADM-6 read literally), query text never logged; (e) children shown as first name + class only; (f) "see all" and result rows link to list screens without the query. Recommended: accept all.
1. Where does the existing 35-table Appwrite schema come from? (Diff against the fresh schema will be added when M01–M04 are done.)
2. Sign off ADRs 0002 and 0004.
3. Appwrite plan: one database only; function execution allowance for a once-a-minute tick?
4. Security: `general_variables.md` in iCloud holds a plaintext GitHub token — revoke/rotate it.
5. SR cites ADR 0013 (reduced pilot scope, 18 Jan 2027); no ticket and no ADR exist for it.
5a. (C04) The plan assumes registration 8 weeks before pilot; the 18 Jan 2027 pilot is already inside that window. Move the pilot, or ask the lawyer whether a closed pilot needs registration first.
6. (D03) Sign off ADRs 0003 and 0012. Default: accept.
7. (D03) Appwrite plan: is Pro $25 per project or per member; do we need Pro for staging? Does the plan give point-in-time recovery (NFR-6)? Default: Pro for prod and staging; ask Appwrite in writing; if no PITR, add a nightly export job.
8. (D03) Engage a Ugandan lawyer/DPO on the DP-7 brief (9 questions in ADR 0012). Default: before the pilot if real children's data is used, otherwise before public launch. If in-country hosting is required, ADRs 0003/0012 are revisited (self-hosted Appwrite).
9. (D03) Create a Fly.io account and a throwaway app, and run the latency script from Kampala on fibre and on MTN and Airtel phones. Default: do it with the F03 staging deploy.
10. (D03) Ledger in Appwrite or Postgres (SR 14 item 2)? Default: keep behind a `LedgerStore` interface on Appwrite for the pilot, decide before automated payouts after a crash-between-postings spike (leaning Postgres).
11. (D03) Fly Standard support ($29/month)? Default: skip for the pilot.
12. (D03) Accept safeguarding lead's blanket read of `audit_log` (deviates from SR §6.2 "Read (cases)")? Default: accept for the pilot, or scope to case-related entries.
13. (D05) Sign off ADR 0005 (Resend). Default: accept.
14. (D05) Which domain sends mail? Default: `mail.<main domain>` subdomain, from `no-reply@` and `safeguarding@`, reply-to `support@<main domain>`. DNS checklist: add Resend's SPF TXT, bounce MX and DKIM records on `mail.<domain>`, then DMARC `v=DMARC1; p=none; rua=mailto:dmarc@<domain>` on `_dmarc.mail.<domain>` (tighten to quarantine/reject after 2-4 weeks of reports); verify in Resend.
15. (D05) Run the inbox test from staging to Gmail, Yahoo and a Ugandan ISP/company address and send the headers (spf/dkim/dmarc pass). Default: fall back to Postmark if any fail.
16. (D06) Sign off ADR 0006 and engage a lawyer on `docs/legal/lawyer-brief.md`. Default: hybrid marketplace positioning (independent teachers, vetting and safeguarding as conditions of use), platform as data controller, register before the pilot if real children's data is used, holiday packages out of MVP until cleared, language "home tutoring" only.

16a. (F06) Admin nav guesses, confirm: superadmin is NOT implicit (sees Overview and Settings only; needs `opsadmin`/`safeguardinglead`/`curator` labels for the rest, per SR 6.1/6.2, A-30 and the F03 core service). Is that right, or should super_admin read everything? Unconfirmed: Overview (all roles), Queues (ops + safeguarding), Matching, Payouts, Reports (ops only), Settings (superadmin only), and no sidebar entry for A-51 Admin users and roles. Also set `ADMIN_COOKIE_SECRET` for staging/prod.
17. (M01) Which schema is the source of truth for the 35 live tables: the repo (`appwrite/schema/*.json`, generated config), the live project, or a merge? Differences for the M01 entities are summarised in the M01 block (table and column names only): live lacks `parent_profile`, `guardian_link`, `home_location`, `child`, `consent_record`; live has table-level label read permissions (against ADR 0012); live `teacher_profile`/`teacher_document`/`teacher_approval`/`policy_acceptance`/`user_profile` have extra or different fields. Default: **merge** with the repo as base (permissions and SR 5.1 entities from the repo, live's richer teacher application, document history and policy-version fields adopted), applied only to a non-live project first; the live project is migrated by the owner, never by an agent. Also decide `user_role` vs `role_assignment` naming (default `role_assignment`) and `first_name`/`last_name` vs `name` (default: split). (M02) The 10 sessions entities (plan, subscription, match_request, match_offer, assignment, session, session_checkin, session_confirmation, cancellation, dispute) do not exist live at all: no diff to reconcile; default: create from the repo. (M04) 8 of the 20 M04 tables exist live (`ledger_account`, `ledger_transaction`, `ledger_entry`, `audit_log`, `notification`, `notification_template`, `notification_preference`, `device_token`) with a different shape and table-level label read permissions (against ADR 0012); the other 12 (tier, tier_history, bonus_rule, invoice, payment, earning, payout_batch, payout, feedback, safeguarding_case, safeguarding_action, case_status) are absent live. Default: merge with the repo as base (details in the M04 block); also decide whether to adopt live's `critical` notification category and `contra_revenue` account type. (M03) 8 of the 19 M03 tables exist live (`curriculum, catalogue_version, level, level_equivalence, subject, strand, topic, struggle_tag`; `curriculum` holds 3 rows) with table-level label read permissions (against ADR 0012), no soft delete, raw uniques and per-version struggle tags; the other 11 (session_log, session_log_topic, session_log_struggle, topic_progress, school_upload, handover_summary, handover_rating, question, assessment_blueprint, assessment, assessment_item) are absent. Default: merge with the repo as base (details in the M03 block); decide whether `struggle_tag` stays unversioned (SR) or per version (live), and whether `strand` stays as an addition beyond SR 5.3 (default: keep, optional on topic).
18. ($02) Confirm MTN Uganda per-transaction min/max in UGX (package guesses 500 and 5,000,000) and the production `X-Target-Environment` (guess `mtnuganda`). Default: keep guesses, configurable. Also confirm pending RequestToPay expiry (polling deadline default 300 s).
19. (M02) Late-cancellation notice threshold is not in the SR: default 24 h (`LATE_CANCELLATION_NOTICE_HOURS`, to become a core-service Setting; needs an F09 inventory row then, and H05 #134 should list it). Late (<24 h) = `cancelled_by_parent_late`, on time = `cancelled_by_parent_ontime`.
20. (M02) Confirm two extensions to the SR 7.4 state machine: `incomplete -> confirmed|void` (admin review outcome) and `no_show` as a terminal state (credit to the parent is a ledger action, not a status change). Default: both accepted.

21. (X01) Travel-time defaults: daily 1,000 / monthly 15,000 Google Routes elements (expected about 7,500 a month for 30 zones x 2 warm bands), alert at 80% and 100%, hard stop at 100%; straight-line fallback 20 km/h x 1.4; time bands 06:30-09:30 / 09:30-15:30 / 15:30-20:00 / night, no weekend peaks. Confirm or change, and say who receives the budget alerts (log sink only until an email/Slack sink is chosen).
22. (A01) Resend cooldown: the issue says 30 s, the brief said 60 s. Default: 30 s (`OTP_RESEND_COOLDOWN_S`).
23. (A01) Anyone with a phone can self-sign-up as `teacher` (label only; no data access; approval is VER-1). Confirm, or require an invitation/application first. Default: allow.
24. (A01) Lockout trade-off: an attacker can lock a victim's number for up to an hour (10 wrong codes) or use up its 5 codes per hour. Default: accept for the pilot; add app attestation (Play Integrity / App Attest) if abused.
25. (A01) Carrier-recycled numbers: a dormant account (180 days) is only flagged in the response and the log. Default: add a step-up (support re-verification) before a payout number change on a dormant account (belongs to the payout tickets). Confirm.
26. (A01) SMS cost per OTP in UGX and a monthly budget alert on the Africa's Talking account (not in SR). Default: set a low balance alert and top up weekly during the pilot.
27. (A01, reviewer) Should phone sign-in be refused (or stepped up) for accounts that carry a privileged label (`opsadmin`, `curator`, `safeguardinglead`, `superadmin`)? Today such a user gets a normal session from the mobile flow without the admin web MFA step. Default: refuse with the generic sign-in error after the code is proven; staff use the admin web app only.

28. (M05) Safeguarding cases: (a) should an UNASSIGNED open case be readable by every safeguarding lead so any lead can triage (`Policy.unassigned_cases_visible_to_leads`, default False: invisible to clients, the queue is a core endpoint)? (b) does the assigned lead keep reading the case record after close (`lead_keeps_closed_case_record`, default True; the related child/address/log/document rows are withdrawn at close either way)?
29. (M05) Address rule: which assignment end reasons withdraw the home address at once (default `parent_cancelled`, `admin`, `teacher_withdrew`, `rematch_requested`; `trial_not_continued`, `subscription_ended`, `trial_converted` keep the 24 h after the last held session)? And is a safeguarding hold an `admin` end? (constant in `policy.py`)
30. (M05) Reference data readable by clients: `plan` (prices) and `zone` are ops-label-only rows today (the apps get them through core endpoints) because ADR 0012 allows `read("users")` only for catalogue content. Allow `read("users")` on them too? Same question for a parent seeing their assigned teacher's `teacher_profile` (bio, photo; there is no teacher display-name column outside `user_profile`, which holds phone/email): default is a core endpoint with a redacted view.
31. (M05) Guardian vocabulary: `guardian_link.grants` (SR calls the column `permissions`) is read as a comma list of `children, logs, sessions, billing, address, consents` or `all`; empty means nothing. Confirm the scopes and what the Phase 2 guardian invite sets by default.
32. (M05) ADR 0012 text to update (decision owner): curators and `notification_preference` are NOT client-writable (zero client writes, everything through the core service); `question` rows are curator+ops only (answer key) while approved media is `read("users")`; audit job reports, reconcile repairs. See the M05 block, "Discrepancies".
33. (M05) Q12 and Q16a (above) are implemented as defaults behind one-line switches (`Policy.audit_log_lead_read=True`; super admin reads only `role_assignment` and `notification_template`). Answer them to freeze or flip.

34. (M05, round 2) The assigned safeguarding lead can read the case-linked `user_profile` (reporter, child's parent, teacher), `parent_profile`, `teacher_profile` and the child's `session` rows while the case is open (withdrawn at close), so the admin global search (H09) works for leads. SR 6.2 gives the lead no own-account/session row. Default: yes, only those rows. If no: drop `parent`/`teacher`/`session` from the lead's search policy in `apps/admin/src/lib/search/policy.ts` and remove the three lines in `rules.SPECS`.
35. (M05, round 2) Staging blocker, not a question: the permission service reads labels from `role_assignment` history until the Appwrite Users label directory is wired behind `LabelDirectory`; confirm that wiring is part of F02/A03 staging work and who owns it (default: M05 follow-up builder before staging).

36. (V01) Minimum weekly availability to apply: default 6 hours (`TEACHER_APP_MIN_WEEKLY_HOURS`). Confirm or change.
37. (V01) Re-apply waiting period after "not approved": default 90 days (`TEACHER_APP_REAPPLY_COOLDOWN_DAYS`). Confirm or change.
38. (V01) Travel limit: the app asks for a radius in km (M01 `travel_radius_km`); SR matching and the live schema use maximum travel time in minutes. Default: keep km for now and switch to minutes (live `max_travel_minutes`) with a schema change before matching is built. Confirm.
39. (V01) Referees: name, phone, relationship, years known, optional email (T-06 says email too); referees are NOT contacted until V04. Is a referee phone enough, or is email mandatory? Default: phone required, email optional. Also confirm the consent sentence and all application copy (copy approval is yours).
40. (V01) Personal data: date of birth (must be 18 or older) and current school are collected; no national ID number (only ID images in V02). Confirm DOB is needed before approval and who may see it (default: owner and ops admin only).
41. (V01) Qualification levels (certificate, diploma, degree, postgraduate diploma, master's, doctorate), relationship list and the language list are my guesses; replace with the real lists. Also: should a graduate be allowed to add a registration number (accepted, optional)? Default yes.
42. (A03) Who may grant which role: staff roles superadmin only (never yourself, system never); parent/guardian/teacher by ops or superadmin or the system. Alternative: ops may also grant `curator`.
43. (A03) Q27 implemented as the default: staff labels get 403 `staff_account` on the phone flow after the code is proven (clear message, not a generic error): is a generic error preferred?
44. (A03) A staff label with no `role_assignment` row (bootstrap) is only reported by `role-reconcile`; `ROLE_RECONCILE_REMOVE_UNRECORDED_STAFF=true` removes it: which for production?
45. (A03) A role staff withdrew (e.g. teacher) is not given back by signing in again; the person must be re-granted by staff: confirm.
46. (A03) Revoking a user's last role leaves the account and profile (they see "No access with this account"); should it also end their sessions (ADR 0012 says "where needed")? Default: no, roles refresh on the next resume.
47. (A03) Bootstrap of the first superadmin (no system actor may grant staff roles): script run by the owner, or a one-off console label plus the reconcile report?
